> उत् । शुष्माः । ओषधीनां । गावः । गोस्थात् इव । ईरते ।  
> धनं । सनिष्यंतीनां । आत्मानं । तव । पुरुष ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ओषधीनां शुष्मा बलान्युदीरते । उद्धच्छंति । रुग्ने स्ववीर्यं प्रोद्गमयंतीत्यर्थः । गावो गोष्ठादिव । ता यथा ततः सकाशादुदीरते तद्वत् । कीदृशीनामोषधीनां । उच्यते । धनं स्वसामर्थ्यलक्षणं सनिष्यंतीनां दातुमिच्छंतीनां।
> किं प्रतीति :उच्यते । पूरुष पुरुष रोगग्रस्त तवात्मानं शरीरं प्रति । यद्वा!
> प्ररोहंतीरोषधीर्दृष्ट्वा वदति । हे पुरुष प्रियंग्वाद्योषधिस्वामिन् तवात्मानं
> वर्धयितुं धनं सनिष्यंतीनां व्रीह्याद्योषधीनां शुष्मा उदीरते ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The powers of the plants rise up; in the sick man they bring forth their own strength. "As cattle from the cattle-pen": as cattle come out from it, so these. Of what plants? Of those that wish to give wealth in the form of their own efficacy. Towards what? Towards you, sick man, towards your body. Or else: seeing the plants sprouting, he says: O man, master of the plants such as priyaṅgu, the powers of the plants such as rice, wishing to bring wealth, rise up to increase your body.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- पुरुष (*puruṣa*)—O sick man
- गोष्ठात् (*goṣṭhāt*)—from the cattle-pen
- गाव इव (*gāva iva*)—as cattle come out
- धनं (*dhanaṃ*)—their wealth of sap
- सनिष्यंतीनां (*saniṣyaṃtīnāṃ*)—wishing to provide
- ओषधीनां (*oṣadhīnāṃ*)—of these plants
- शुष्माः (*śuṣmāḥ*)—the powers
- तव (*tava*)—your
- आत्मानं (*ātmānaṃ*)—towards the body
- उदीरते (*udīrate*)—come out, manifesting

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O sick man, like cattle coming out of the pen, the powers of these plants, wishing to provide their wealth of sap, come out manifest towards your body.

### Printed p. 82

##### English translation
The virtues of the plants which are desirous of bestowing wealth issue from them, man, (towards) your body like cattle from the pen.

#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इष्कृ॑ति॒र्नाम॑ वो मा॒ताथो॑ यू॒यं स्थ॒ निष्कृ॑तीः ।  
> सी॒राः प॑त॒त्रिणीः॑ स्तन॒ यदा॒मय॑ति॒ निष्कृ॑थ ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इष्कृतिः । नाम । वः । माता । अथो इति । यूयं । स्थ । निःकृतीः ।  
> सीराः । पतत्रिणीः । स्थन । यत् । आमयति । निः । कृथ ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे ओषधयः वो माता जननीष्कृतिर्नाम । सर्वेषां रुग्णानां निष्कर्तीति
> प्रसिद्धा यस्मात्सा रुग्णं निष्करोति । अथातो यूयमपि निष्कृतीर्निष्कृतयः स्थ ।
> भवथ । किंच यूयं सीराः सरणशीलाः पतत्रिणीः पतनवत्यश्च स्थन । भवथ ॥ तप्तनबिति
> तनादेशः ॥ किंच पुरुषो यद्यद्यामयति व्याधितो भवति तं निष्कृथ । संस्कुरुथ ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O plants, your mother is called Iṣkṛti, famed as the one who cures all the sick, since she cures the sick. Therefore you too are Niṣkṛtis. Moreover you are moving (सीराः (*sīrāḥ*)) and able to fly (पतत्रिणीः (*patatriṇīḥ*)); स्थन (*sthana*) stands for "you are", by the substitution of तन (*tana*) for *taptanap*. And whenever a man falls ill, you cure him (निष्कृथ (*niṣkṛtha*): you restore him).**NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O plants) वः (*vaḥ*)—your
- माता (*mātā*)—mother
- इष्कृतिः नाम (*iṣkṛtiḥ nāma*)—is famed as Iṣkṛti, because she removes all diseases
- अथ (*atha*)—therefore
- यूयं (*yūyaṃ*)—you also
- निष्कृतीः स्थ (*niṣkṛtīḥ stha*)—for that very reason are famed as Niṣkṛtis
- पतत्रिणीः (*patatriṇīḥ*)—able to fly
- सीराः (*sīrāḥ*)—of moving nature
- स्थन (*sthana*)—you are
- यत् (*yat*)—if
- आमयति (*āmayati*)—a man becomes ill
- निष्कृथ (*niṣkṛtha*)—cure him

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O plants, your mother is famed as Iṣkṛti because she removes all diseases. Therefore you too, for this very reason, are famed as Niṣkṛtis. You are also able to fly. If a man is afflicted by disease, cure him.

### Printed p. 83

##### English translation
Verily Ishkriti is your mother, therefore are you (also)Nishkritis; you are flying streams; if (aman) is ill you cure him.

#### Ṛk 10

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अति॒ विश्वाः॑ परि॒ष्ठाः स्ते॒न इ॑व व्र॒जम॑क्रमुः ।  
> ओष॑धीः॒ प्राचु॑च्यवु॒र्यत्किं च॑ त॒न्नो॒३॒॑३रपः॑ ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अति । विश्वाः ! परिसस्थाः । स्तेनः इव । व्रजं । अक्रमुः ।  
> ओषधीः । प्र । अचुच्यवुः । यत् । किं । च । तन्वः । रपः ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> विश्वा व्याप्ताः परिष्ठाः परितः स्थिता ओषधयोऽत्यक्रमुः । व्याधीनतिक्रांतवत्यः।
> स्तेन इव व्रजं । यथा स्तेनो व्रजमत्यक्रमीत् तद्वत् । तथा कृत्वाषधीरोषधयः
> प्राचुच्यवुः प्रच्यावयंति यत्किं च तन्नो रुग्णशरीरस्य रपः पापं व्याधिलक्षणमस्ति
> तदिति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The plants, pervading (विश्वा (*viśvā*)) and standing all around, have overcome the diseases, as a thief goes over a cow-pen. Having so done, the plants drive out whatever sin of the sick body, in the form of disease, there is.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- स्तेनः (*stenaḥ*)—a thief
- व्रजं इव (*vrajaṃ iva*)—as he enters the cow-pen and carries off all the cows
- परिष्ठाः (*pariṣṭhāḥ*)—pervading all around
- विश्वाः ओषधीः (*viśvāḥ oṣadhīḥ*)—all plants
- अत्यक्रमुः (*atyakramuḥ*)—assail the disease
- तन्वः (*tanvaḥ*)—of the body
- यत्किं च रपः (*yatkiṃ ca rapaḥ*)—whatever fault there is
- प्राचुच्यवुः (*prācucyavuḥ*)—drive out

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

As a thief enters a cow-pen and carries off all the cows, all the plants pervading all around assail the disease and carry it off. Whatever fault of the body there is, they drive it out.

##### English translation
The universal all-pervading plants assail (diseases) as a thief (attacks) a cow-shed; they drive out whatever infirmity of body there may be.

### Printed p. 84
#### Ṛk 11

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यदि॒मा वा॒जय॑न्न॒हमोष॑धी॒र्हस्त॑ आद॒धे ।  
> आ॒त्मा यक्ष॑स्य नश्यति पु॒रा जी॑व॒गृभो॑ यथा ॥ ११ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यत् । इमाः । नाजयन् । अहं । ओषधीः । हस्ते । आदधे ।  
> आत्मा । यक्षस्य । नश्यति । पुरा । जीवगृभः । यथा ॥ ११ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अहं यद्यदीमा ओषधीर्हस्त आदधे आधारयामि । किं कुर्वन् । वाजयन्
> रुग्णं बलिनं कुर्वन्। ततः पुरा यक्षस्य रोगस्यात्मा नश्यति नष्टो भवति जीवगृभो
> यथा । जीवानां शकुनादीनां ग्राहकाद्व्याधाद्यथा जीवा नश्यंति तद्वत् । यद्वा।
> जीवगृभो मृत्योः सकाशाज्जीवोऽपह्रियते । तद्वत् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

If I hold these plants in my hand, making the sick man strong (वाजयन् (*vājayan*)), then before that the self of the यक्ष (*yakṣa*) disease perishes, as living creatures perish before the जीवगृभ (*jīvagṛbha*), the hunter who seizes living things such as birds. Or else: as life is carried off from the presence of death, the seizer of life.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- जीवगृभो यथा (*jīvagṛbho yathā*)—as the life of birds and the like flies away as soon as the hunter seizes them
- अहं (*ahaṃ*)—I
- यत् (*yat*)—when
- वाजयन् (*vājayan*)—making the sick man strong
- इमाः ओषधीः (*imāḥ oṣadhīḥ*)—these plants
- हस्ते (*haste*)—in the hand
- आदधे (*ādadhe*)—I hold, then
- पुरा (*purā*)—even before applying them
- यक्ष्मस्य (*yakṣmasya*)—of the disease
- आत्मा (*ātmā*)—the strength
- नश्यति (*naśyati*)—perishes

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

As the life of birds and the like flies away as soon as the hunter seizes them, when I hold these plants in my hand to make the sick man strong, then even before applying them the strength of the disease perishes.

##### English translation
As soon as I take these plants in my hand making (the sick man) strong, the soul of the malady perishes before (their application) as (life is driven away from the persence) of the seizer of life.

### Printed p. 85

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In explaining the particle यथा (*yathā*) in its sense of comparison, Yāska takes the second half of this Ṛk as an example and gives its etymology: आत्मा यक्षस्य नश्यति पुरा जीवगृभो यथा । आत्माततेर्वाप्तेर्वापि वाप्त इव स्यात् यावद्व्याप्तिभूत इति ॥ (*ātmā yakṣasya naśyati purā jīvagṛbho yathā | ātmātatervāptervāpi vāpta iva syāt yāvadvyāptibhūta iti ‖*) (Ni. 3-15).

As a hunter, before he has even gripped a bird firmly in his hand, finds it dead from fear, so, as soon as I hold the plant in my hand, that is, even before applying it to the body, the ātman of the यक्ष (*yakṣa*) disease, that is, its root (cause), is at once destroyed. The meaning is that its very root is destroyed so that the disease does not return.

The word आत्मा (*ātmā*) is derived either from the root आत् (*āt*), meaning "go", or from the root आप् (*āp*) or व्याप् (*vyāp*), meaning "pervade and spread". How: since this self is in all beings and in all places, it is called आत्मा (*ātmā*); or, because it pervades everything, it is called आत्मा (*ātmā*). Or, since it is customary to say that it pervades only a particular place, that is, only the body of a creature, it is called आत्मा (*ātmā*). Though the self pervades the whole universe collectively (samaṣṭi), it is said to be in the bodies of creatures individually (vyaṣṭi, as separate in each). Therefore it is subject to the two kinds of pervasion, आप्ति (*āpti*) and व्याप्ति (*vyāpti*). आप्ति (*āpti*) means that its pervasion in the whole universe is collective. व्याप्ति (*vyāpti*) means that it is customary to say that it is pervading (dwelling) in the body of one particular creature (or man). Because it is subject to these two kinds of pervasion, it has the name आत्मा (*ātmā*): this is the purport of the etymology.

#### Ṛk 12

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यस्यौ॑षधीः प्र॒सर्प॒थांग॑मंगं॒ परु॑ष्परुः ।  
> ततो॒ यक्ष्मं॒ वि बा॑धध्व उ॒ग्रो म॑ध्यम॒शीरि॑व ॥ १२ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यस्य । ओषधीः । प्रसर्पथ । अंगं5अंगं । परुः5परुः ।  
> ततः । यक्ष्यं । वि । बाधद्वे । उग्रः । मध्यमशी5इव ॥ १२ ॥

### Printed p. 86
#### Ṛk 12 (continuing)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे ओषधीरोषधयः यस्य रुग्णस्यांगमंगं यद्यदंगं परुःपरुर्यद्यत्पर्व प्रसर्पथ प्रकर्षेणाश्रयथ ततोऽंगात्पर्वणश्च यक्ष्यं व्याधिं वि बाधद्वे ।
> उग्र उद्धूर्णबलो मध्यमशीर्मध्यमस्थाने वर्तमानो राजा यथोपद्रवकारिणः समनंतरं शत्रुवेदेपदे विबाधते तद्वत् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O plants, in whatever sick man's every limb and every joint you creep, you enter it thoroughly; from that limb and joint drive away the disease. Like a mighty king, of great strength, stationed in the middle, who drives away troublemakers when enemies come close, so drive it out."

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ओषधीः (*oṣadhīḥ*)—O plants
- उग्रः (*ugraḥ*)—mighty
- मध्यमशीः इव (*madhyamaśīḥ iva*)—as a king standing and fighting in the middle of his army destroys all his enemies
- यूयं (*yūyaṃ*)—you
- यस्य (*yasya*)—of whatever sick man
- अंगंअंगं (*aṃgaṃaṃgaṃ*)—of every limb
- परुः परुः (*paruḥ paruḥ*)—all the joint (junction) regions
- प्रसर्पथ (*prasarpatha*)—when you enter and spread
- ततः (*tataḥ*)—from all those regions
- यक्ष्यं (*yakṣyaṃ*)—the disease
- वि बाधद्वे (*vi bādhadve*)—destroy completely

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O plants, just as a mighty king, fighting in the midst of his army, destroys all his enemies, so, in whatever sick man you enter and spread into all the regions of every limb, from all those regions destroy his disease completely.

##### English translation
From him, oh plants, in whom you creep from limb to limb, from joint to joint you drive away disease like a mighty (prince) stationed in the midst of his host.

#### Ṛk 13

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> सा॒कं य॑क्ष्म॒ प्र प॑त॒ चाषे॑ण किकिदी॒विना॑ ।  
> सा॒कं वात॑स्य॒ ध्राज्या॑ सा॒कं न॑श्य नि॒हाक॑या ॥ १३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> साकं । यक्ष्य । प्र । पत । चाषेण । किकिदीविना ।  
> साकं । वातस्य । ध्राज्या । साकं । नश्य । निहाकया ॥ १३ ॥

### Printed p. 87

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अस्मदीयस्य पुरुषस्य शरीराधिष्ठायिन्नृष्म व्याधे त्वं साकं सहैव प्र
> पत । प्रकर्षेण शीघ्रं गच्छ । केन साकमिति उच्यते । चाषेणातिशीघ्रं पतता
> चाषाख्येन पक्षिणा सह । तथा किकिदीविना पक्षिणा च सह । तथा वातस्य शीघ्रं गच्छतो
> वायोर्ध्राज्या ॥ ध्रज गत् ॥ गतिवेगेन सह गच्छ । तथा निहाकया गोधिकया
> साकं नश्य । नाशं प्राप्नुहि ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O disease abiding in the body of our man, go forth quickly together with something: with what? With the bird called Cāṣa, which flies very swiftly; likewise with the bird Kikidīvi; likewise go with the speed of the swiftly moving wind (ध्रज गत् (*dhraja gat*) is the root of ध्राज्या (*dhrājyā*), speed of motion). Likewise perish along with the निहाकया (*nihākayā*), the Godhikā (iguana); attain destruction."

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यक्ष्म (*yakṣma*)—O disease
- साकं (*sākaṃ*)—at one time
- प्रपत (*prapata*)—be ruined and go
- चाषेण (*cāṣeṇa*)—with the speed of the Cāṣa bird
- किकिदीविना साकं (*kikidīvinā sākaṃ*)—go off also with the Kikidīvi bird
- वातस्य (*vātasya*)—of the wind
- ध्राज्या (*dhrājyā*)—with the speed
- निहाकयो साकं (*nihākayo sākaṃ*)—with the storm as well
- नश्य (*naśya*)—vanish

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O disease, go off with the swiftly flying Cāṣa bird; go off with the Kikidivi bird; vanish with the speed of the wind and with the storm.

##### English translation
Fly forth, sickness, with the jay, with the blue jay, with the velocity of the wind, persh along with the iguana.

#### Ṛk 14

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒न्या वो॑ अ॒न्याम॑वत्व॒न्यान्यस्या॒ उपा॑वत ।  
> ताः सर्वाः॑ संविदा॒ना इ॒दं मे॒ प्राव॑ता॒ वचः॑ ॥ १४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अन्या । वः । अन्यां । अवतु । अन्या । अन्यस्याः । उप । अवत ।  
> ताः । सर्वाः । संविदानाः । इदं । मे । प्र । अवत । वचः ॥ १४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे ओषधयः वो युष्माकं मध्येऽन्यौषधिरन्यामोषधिमवतु । प्राप्नोतु ।
> अवतिरत्र गत्यर्थः । तथान्यान्यस्याः समीपमुपावत । उपगच्छत । एवं याः संति

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O plants, among you let one plant 'protect' another, that is, reach it; अवति (*avati*) here has the sense of going. Likewise let each go to the vicinity of another. So all the plants that are on earth, being संविदानाः (*saṃvidānāḥ*)—
### Printed p. 88
agreed with one another in unanimity—protect this speech of mine, this speech in the form of a prayer."

> क्षित्यामोषधयः ताः सर्वाः संविदानाः परस्परमैकमत्यं प्राप्ताः सत्य इदं मे
> मदीयं वचः प्रार्थनालक्षणं वचनं प्रावत । प्ररक्षत ॥

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वः (*vaḥ*)—among you (O plants)
- अन्या (*anyā*)—one plant
- अन्यां (*anyāṃ*)—another
- अवतु (*avatu*)—let it approach
- अन्यान्यस्याः (*anyānyasyāḥ*)—mutually, one in the vicinity of another
- उपावत (*upāvata*)—go and join
- ताः सर्वाः (*tāḥ sarvāḥ*)—all the plants thus
- संविदानाः (*saṃvidānāḥ*)—joining together with one another
- मे (*me*)—my
- इदं वचः (*idaṃ vacaḥ*)—this prayer-utterance
- प्रावत (*prāvata*)—heed

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O plants, let one plant among you approach another. All of you, joining together, heed this prayer of mine.

##### English translation
Let each of you, plants, go to the other, approach the one (to the vicinity) of the other; thus being all mutually joined together, attend to this my speeeeh.

#### Ṛk 15

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> याः फ॒लिनी॒र्या अ॑फ॒ला अ॑पु॒ष्पा याश्च॑ पु॒ष्पिणीः॑ ।  
> बृह॒स्पति॑प्रसूता॒स्ता नो॑ मुंचं॒त्वंह॑सः ॥ १५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> याः । फलिनीः । याः । अफलाः । अपुष्पाः । याः । च । पुष्पिणीः ।  
> बृहस्पतिप्रसूताः । ताः । नः । मुंचंतु । अंहसः ॥ १५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> याः फलिनीः फलवत्यो या अफलाः फलवर्जिता या अपुष्पाः पुष्परहिता
> याश्च पुष्टिणीः पुष्पवत्यो बृहस्पतिप्रसूताः । बृहस्पतिर्मंत्राभिमानी देवः ।
> तेनानुज्ञाताः । ता नोऽस्मानंहसो मुंचंतु । मोचयंतु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"Those that are fruit-bearing, those without fruit, those without flowers and those with flowers are बृहस्पतिप्रसूताः (*bṛhaspatiprasūtāḥ*): Bṛhaspati is the god presiding over the mantra, and they are permitted by him. May they release us from sin (अंहसः (*aṃhasaḥ*))."**NOTE**

### Printed p. 89

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- बृहस्पतिप्रसूताः (*bṛhaspatiprasūtāḥ*)—among the plants that are the objects of Bṛhaspati's favour
- याः (*yāḥ*)—which
- फलिनीः (*phalinīḥ*)—are fruit-bearing
- याः (*yāḥ*)—which
- अफलाः (*aphalāḥ*)—are fruitless
- अपुष्पाः (*apuṣpāḥ*)—which are flowerless
- याश्च (*yāśca*)—and which
- पुष्पिणीः (*puṣpiṇīḥ*)—possess flowers
- ताः (*tāḥ*)—all of them
- नः (*naḥ*)—us
- अंहसः (*aṃhasaḥ*)—from sin in the form of disease
- मुंचंतु (*muṃcaṃtu*)—let them release

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Among the plants that are the objects of Bṛhaspati's favour, those which bear fruit, those which are fruitless, those which are flowerless and those which have flowers—may all of them release us from sin in the form of disease.

##### English translation
Whether bearing fruit or barren, whether flowering or flowerless, may they, the progeny of Brihaspathi, liberate us from sin.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

फलिनीः अफलाः पुष्पिणीः अपुष्पाः (*phalinīḥ aphalāḥ puṣpiṇīḥ apuṣpāḥ*)—It is well known that there are many kinds of plants. In some kinds fruit is borne; in some kinds there are no fruits. Likewise in some kinds there are flowers; in some kinds there are no flowers either. This is the meaning.

#### Ṛk 16

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> मुं॒चंतु॑ मा शप॒थ्या॒३॒॑सिदथो॑ वरु॒ण्य॑दु॒त ।  
> अथो॑ य॒मस्य॒ पद्मी॑शा॒त्सर्व॑स्माद्देवकिल्बि॒षात् ॥ १६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> मुंचंतु । मा । शपथ्यात् । अथो इति । वरुण्यात् । उत ।  
> अथो इति । यमस्य । पड्बीशात् । सर्वस्मात् । देवकिल्पिषात् ॥ १६ ॥

### Printed p. 90

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> मा मानोषधयः शपथ्याच्छपथसंजातादेनसः सकाशान्मुंचंतु । अथो
> अपि च वरुण्याद्वरुणसंभवान्मां मुंचंतु । वरुणोऽपि स्वपाशेन जातमात्रं
> पुरुषं बध्नाति । उतेति पूरणः । अथो अपि च यमस्य पद्मीशात्पादबंधनान्निगडान्मुंचंतु । न केवलं वरुणादेः पापात् किंतु सर्वस्त्यादेवकिल्पिषाद्देवैः
> कृतात्पापान्मुंचंतु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"May the plants release me from the sin born of a curse (शपथ्यात् (*śapathyāt*)); and also from that arising from Varuṇa, for Varuṇa binds a man with his noose as soon as he is born. उत (*uta*) is a filler. And also may they release me from Yama's पद्मीशात् (*padmīśāt*), the fetter binding the feet. Not only from the sin of Varuṇa and others, but from all देवकिल्पिषात् (*devakilpiṣāt*), sin committed by the gods, may they release me."**NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ओषधयः (*oṣadhayaḥ*)—the plants
- मा (*mā*)—me
- शपथ्यात् (*śapathyāt*)—from sin arising from a curse
- मुंचंतु (*muṃcaṃtu*)—let them release
- वरुण्यात् (*varuṇyāt*)—from the trouble (noose) arising from Varuṇa
- मां (*māṃ*)—me, let them release
- अथो (*atho*)—moreover
- यमस्यॆ (*yamasyè*)—of Yama
- पड्बीशात् (*paḍbīśāt*)—from the noose that binds the feet, let them release
- सर्वस्मात् (*sarvasmāt*)—from all
- देवकिल्बिसात् (*devakilbisāt*)—from the fault connected with the gods, let them release

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

May the plants make me free from the sin arising from a curse. May they release me from Varuṇa's noose. May they release me from Yama's noose that binds the feet. May they release me also from every fault connected with the gods.

##### English translation
May they liberate me from the sin produced by curse, from the sin caused by Varuna, from the fetters of Yama, from all guilt caused by the gods.

#### Ṛk 17

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒व॒पतं॑तीरवदन्नि॒व ओष॑धय॒स्सरि॑ ।  
> यं जी॒वम॒श्नवा॑मुहै॒ न स रि॑ष्याति॒ पूरु॑षः ॥ १७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अवपतंतीः । अवदन् । दिवः । ओषधयः । परि ।  
> यं । जीवं । अश्वनामहै । न । सः । रिष्याति । पुरुषः ॥ १७ ॥

### Printed p. 91

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> दिवो द्युलोकादवपतंतीरवपतंत्य ओषधय इत्थं पर्यवदन् । किमिति
> उच्यते । यं जीवं जीवंतमत्नवामहै व्याप्नुमो न स पूरुषः पुरुषो
> रिष्याति । रिष्यति । विनश्यति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"The plants, descending from heaven, the world of light, spoke thus. What? 'The living man whom we pervade — that man does not suffer harm; रिष्याति (*riṣyāti*) is रिष्यति (*riṣyati*), he perishes.'"**NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- दिवः (*divaḥ*)—from the heavenly world
- अवपतंतीः (*avapataṃtīḥ*)—descending to earth
- ओषधयः (*oṣadhayaḥ*)—the plants
- पर्यवदन् (*paryavadan*)—have spoken thus
- जीवं (*jīvaṃ*)—living
- यं (*yaṃ*)—whichever man
- अश्वनामहै (*aśvanāmahai*)—we enter
- सः पूरुषः (*saḥ pūruṣaḥ*)—that man
- न रिष्याति (*na riṣyāti*)—does not meet destruction

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The plants descending from heaven to earth have given this assurance: "Whatever living man we enter, for him there is certainly no destruction."

##### English translation
The plants, falling from heaven, said, " The man whom living we pervade, will not perish."

#### Ṛk 18

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> या ओष॑धीः॒ सोम॑राज्ञीर्ब॒ह्वीः श॒तवि॑चक्षणाः ।  
> तासां॒ त्वम॑स्युत्त॒मारं॒ कामा॑य॒ शं हृ॒दे ॥ १८ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> याः । ओषधीः । सोमराज्ञीः । बह्वीः । शतविचक्षणाः ।  
> तासां । त्वं । असि । उत्तमा । अरं । कामाय । शं । हृदे ॥ १८ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> या ओषधीरोषधयः सोमराज्ञे सोमो राजा स्वामी यासां
> तास्तथोक्ता बह्वीरसंख्याताः शतविचक्षणा बहुदर्शना हे सोमाख्य ओषदे
> तासामोषधीनां त्वमुत्तमासि । यस्मादेवं तस्मादरमलमत्यर्थं कामय
> कांताय हृदे हृदयाय शं सुखकरी भवेति शेषः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"The plants whose king (lord) is Soma, those that are many, innumerable, शतविचक्षणा (*śatavicakṣaṇā*), of many appearances: O plant called Soma, you are the best of those plants. Since this is so, be अरं (*araṃ*), abundantly, pleasant to the beloved heart (कामाय (*kāmāya*) read as काम (*kāma*), कांताय हृदे (*kāṃtāya hṛde*)), is the word to be supplied: 'be happiness-giving'."**NOTE**

### Printed p. 92

*Pratipadārtha and Bhāvārtha of the preceding Ṛk (continuing)*

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- बह्वीः (*bahvīḥ*)—numerous
- शतविचक्षणाः (*śatavicakṣaṇāḥ*)—possessed of the power of manifold seeing
- या ओषधीः (*yā oṣadhīḥ*)—which plants
- सोमराष्ट्रेः (*somarāṣṭreḥ*)—those that have Soma himself as their king
- अरं (*araṃ*)—fully
- कामाय (*kāmāya*)—as the one who grants our desired ends
- हृदे (*hṛde*)—to our heart
- शं (*śaṃ*)—be the bringer of happiness
- तासां (*tāsāṃ*)—among them
- त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you
- उत्तमा असि (*uttamā asi*)—you are the best (therefore…)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O plant called Soma, of those plants which are numerous, which have the power of manifold seeing, and which have Soma himself as their king, you are the best. Therefore be fully the giver of our desired ends and the bringer of happiness to our heart.

##### English translation
The plants which have the Soma for their king, and are numerous and all-seeking, of them you (O Soma-plant) are the best ; be very bountiful to the affectionate heart.

#### Ṛk 19

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> या ओष॑धीः॒ सोम॑राज्ञ॒र्विष्ठि॑ताः पृथि॒वीमनु॑ ।  
> बृह॒स्पति॑प्रसूता अ॒स्मै सं द॑त्त वी॒र्यं॑ ॥ १९ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> याः । ऒषधीः । सोमराज्ञे । विस्तिता । पृथिवीं । अनु ।  
> बृहस्पतिप्रसूताः । अस्मै । सं । दत्त । वीर्यं ॥ १९ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> या ओषधीरोपधयः सोमराज्ञे पृथिवीमनु विष्मिता विविधं स्थिताः दिवः
> सकाशादागत्य पृथिव्यां नानाभेदेन स्थिताः बृहस्पतिप्रसूता बृहस्पतिनाहुज्माशाः
> क्षो यूयमस्यै रुग्धतत्वे वीर्यं सं दत्त । संधत्त ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The plants which, for Soma the king, are spread out variously over the earth: having come from the region of heaven they stand on earth in manifold divisions. Sāyaṇa then explains *Bṛhaspati-impelled* (बृहस्पतिप्रसूता (*bṛhaspatiprasūtā*)) as sent forth by Bṛhaspati **NOTE**. "You give vigour to this sick body"; सं दत्त (*saṃ datta*) is explained as संधत्त (*saṃdhatta*).

### Printed p. 93

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ओषधीः (*oṣadhīḥ*)—O plants
- याः (*yāḥ*)—you who
- सोमराज्ञीः (*somarājñīḥ*)—having Soma himself as king
- पृथिवीं (*pṛthivīṃ*)—the earth
- अनु विष्ठिताः (*anu viṣṭhitāḥ*)—pervading
- बृहस्पतिप्रसूताः (*bṛhaspatiprasūtāḥ*)—having received the favour of Bṛhaspati; such as you are
- अस्यै (*asyai*)—to this sick person
- वीर्यं (*vīryaṃ*)—strength
- सं दत्त (*saṃ datta*)—supply

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O plants, you who have Soma as your king, who pervade the earth, and who have received the favour of Bṛhaspati, fill this sick person with strength.

##### English translation
Plants, which have the Soma for your king, who are scattered over the earth, the offspring of Brihaspathi, give vigour to this (infirm body.)

#### Ṛk 20

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> मा पो॑ रिषत्पनि॒ता यस्मै॑ चा॒हं खना॑मि वः ।  
> द्वि॒पच्चतु॑ष्पद॒स्माकं॒ सर्व॑मस्तृनातु॒रं ॥ २० ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> मा । वः । रिषत् । खनिता । यस्मै । च । अहं । खनामि । वः ।  
> द्विपत् । चतुःपत् । अस्माकं । सर्वं । अस्तु । अनातुरं ॥ २० ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे ओषधयः वो युष्मान्मा रिषत् । मा हिंस्यात् । कः । खनिता भूमेः
> खननकर्ता । यस्मै रुग्णाय चाहं खनामि वो युष्मान् । किंचास्माकं संबंधि
> द्विपत् पुत्रभृत्यादिकं चतुष्पद्दोमहिष्यादिकं च यदस्ति तत्सर्वमनातुरमरोगमस्तु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O plants, let not the digger of the earth harm you, nor I, for whatever sick person I dig you up. Moreover, whatever belongs to us, the biped (sons, servants and the like) and the quadruped (cows, buffaloes and the like), let all of it be free from disease.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- खनिता (*khanitā*)—he who digs the earth for you
- वः (*vaḥ*)—you
- मा रिषत् (*mā riṣat*)—let him not harm
- यस्मै (*yasmai*)—for whatever sick person
- अहं (*ahaṃ*)—I
- खनामि (*khanāmi*)—dig (even then)

### Printed p. 94

(continuing) let me not harm you either.
- वः (*vaḥ*)—you
- अस्माकं (*asmākaṃ*)—belonging to us
- द्विपत् (*dvipat*)—sons and the like
- चतुष्पात् (*catuṣpāt*)—cattle and the like
- सर्वं (*sarvaṃ*)—all living beings
- अनातुरं (*anāturaṃ*)—free from disease
- अस्तु (*astu*)—let be

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O plants, let him who digs the earth to obtain you not harm you; and when I dig for the sake of a sick person, let me too not harm you. Let our sons and the like, our cattle and the like, and all living beings be free from disease.

##### English translation
Let not the digger hurt you, nor (the sick person) for whom I dig you up; may all my bipeds and quadrupeds be free from disease.

#### Ṛk 21

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> याश्चे॒दमु॑पशृ॒ण्वंति॒ याश्च॑ दू॒रं परा॑गताः ।  
> सर्वाः॑ सं॒गत्य॑ वीरुधो॒ऽस्यॆ सं द॑त्त वी॒र्यं॑ ॥ २१ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> याः । च । इदं । उपशृण्वंति । याः । च । दूरं । परागताः ।  
> सर्वाः । संगत्य । वीरुधः । अस्यै । सं । दत्त । वीर्यं ॥ २१ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> याश्चौषधय इदं स्तोत्रमुपशृण्वंति याश्चौषधयो दूरं परागताः सर्वा
> वीरुधः संगत्य संगताः सत्यो हे वीरुधः अस्यै रुग्णतन्वे वीर्यं सं दत्त ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The plants that hear this hymn of praise, and the plants that have gone far away: all these creepers (वीरुधः (*vīrudhaḥ*)), having come together, O creepers, give vigour to this sick body.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वीरुधः (*vīrudhaḥ*)—O plants, (among you)
- याः (*yāḥ*)—which plants
- इदं (*idaṃ*)—this hymn of praise
- उपशृण्वंति (*upaśṛṇvaṃti*)—are able to hear
- याः च (*yāḥ ca*)—and which
- दूरं परागताः (*dūraṃ parāgatāḥ*)—are hidden in a far-off region
- सर्वाः (*sarvāḥ*)—all of you
- संगत्य (*saṃgatya*)—having come together
- अस्यॆ (*asyè*)—to the body of this sick person
- वीर्यं (*vīryaṃ*)—strength
- सं दत्त (*saṃ datta*)—supply

### Printed p. 95

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O plants, those of you that are near and can hear this hymn, and those that are hidden in a far-off region, all of you come together and supply strength to the body of this sick person.

##### English translation
Both the plants that hear this (prayer), and those which are removed far off, all comming together, give vigour to this (infirm body).

#### Ṛk 22

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ओष॑धयः॒ सं व॑दंते॒ सोमे॑न स॒ह राज्ञा॑ ।  
> यस्मै॑ कृ॒णोति॑ ब्राह्म॒णस्तं रा॑जन्पारयामसि ॥ २२ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ओषधयः । सं । वदंते । सोमेन । सह । राज्णा ।  
> यस्मै । कृणोति । ब्राह्मणः । तं । राजन् । पारयामसि ॥ २२ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ओषधयः सर्वाः सोमेन राज्ञा सह सं वदंते । संवादं कुर्वंति । किमिति
> उच्यते । यस्मै रुग्णाय ब्राह्मण ओषधिसामर्थ्यज्ञो ब्राह्मणो वैद्यः कृणोति
> करोति चिकित्सा० तं रुग्णं हे राजन् पारयामसि । पारयामः ॥ इदंतो मसिः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

All the plants converse with Soma the king; they hold a dialogue. What do they say? For whatever sick person the brāhmaṇa, the physician who knows the power of the plants, performs treatment, that sick person, O king, we bring through (पारयामसि (*pārayāmasi*) = पारयामः (*pārayāmaḥ*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ओषधयः (*oṣadhayaḥ*)—all the plants
- राज्ञा (*rājñā*)—with the lord
- सोमेन सह (*somena saha*)—with the god Soma
- सं वदंते (*saṃ vadaṃte*)—speak thus
- राजन् (*rājan*)—O lord
- यस्मै (*yasmai*)—for whichever sick person
- ब्राह्मणः (*brāhmaṇaḥ*)—the physician who knows the nature of the plants
- कृणोति (*kṛṇoti*)—performs treatment
- तं (*taṃ*)—that sick person
- पारयामसि (*pārayāmasi*)—we shall cure

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

All the plants, gathering together, speak thus to their lord, the god Soma: "O lord, the sick person for whom the physician who knows the nature of the plants performs treatment, that sick person we shall cure."

### Printed p. 96

##### English translation
All the plants, together with Soma their king, declare, " We save him, O king, to whom the brahman administers (us)".

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

ब्राह्मणः (*brāhmaṇaḥ*): brahma means mantra; one who knows it is a brāhmaṇa. Here the word brāhmaṇa can be given two kinds of meaning: a physician who also knows the nature of the mantra, or a brāhmaṇa.

#### Ṛk 23

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> त्वमु॑त्त॒मास्यो॑षधे॒ तव॑ वृ॒क्षा उप॑स्तयः ।  
> उप॑स्तिरस्तु॒ सो॒३॒॑ऽस्माकं॒ यो अ॒स्मा अ॑भि॒दास॑ति ॥ २३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> त्वं । उत्तमा । असि । ओषधे । तव । वृक्षाः । उपस्तयः ।  
> उपस्तिः । अस्तु । सः । अस्माकं । यः । अस्मान् । अभिदासति ॥ २३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे ओषधे सोमाख्ये त्वमोषधीनामन्यासामुत्तमासि । तव वृक्षाः सर्व
> उपस्तयः । अधः शायिन एव । तथा सति स उपस्थिरस्तु अधःशायी भवतु योऽस्मानभिदासति हिनस्ति । भ्रातृव्य इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O plant, called Soma, you are the best of the other plants. All trees are your उपस्तयः (*upastayaḥ*), that is, lying below (you). That being so, let him be उपस्थिरस्तु (*upasthirastu*), lying below, who attacks (अभिदासति (*abhidāsati*), harms) us; he is the enemy (भ्रातृव्य (*bhrātṛvya*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ओषधे (*oṣadhe*)—O plant of the form of Soma
- त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you
- उत्तमा असि (*uttamā asi*)—are the best among all plants
- तव (*tava*)—before you
- वृक्षाः (*vṛkṣāḥ*)—all trees
- उपस्तयः (*upastayaḥ*)—are bowed down
- यः (*yaḥ*)—whoever
- नः (*naḥ*)—us
- अभिदासति (*abhidāsati*)—harms
- सः (*saḥ*)—he
- अस्माकं (*asmākaṃ*)—to us
- उपस्थिः (*upasthiḥ*)—bowing down
- अस्तु (*astu*)—let him be

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O plant of the form of Soma, you are the best among all plants. All trees are bowed before you. Whoever harms us, let him bow to us and be subject to us.

### Printed p. 97

##### English translation
You (soma) are the best of the plants, to you (all) trees are prostrate; may he be prostrate to us, who attacks us.

## Sūkta 98 (Maṇḍala 10)

Concerning this sūkta, Yāska, the author of the Nirukta, has told an ancient story:

अत्रेतिहासमाचक्षते । देवापिश्चार्ष्टिषेणः शंतनुश्च कौरव्यौ भ्रातरा बभूवतुः । स शंतुनुः कनीयानभिषेचयांचक्रे । देवापिस्तपः प्रतिपेदे । ततः शंतनो राज्ये द्वादश वर्षाणि देवो न ववर्ष । तमूचुब्राह्मणा अधर्मस्त्वया चरितो ज्येष्ठं भ्रातरमंतरित्याभिषेचितं तस्मात्ते देवो न वर्षतीति । स शंतुनुर्देवापिं शिशिक्ष राज्येन । तमुवाच देवापिः पुरोहितस्तेऽसानि याजयानि च त्वेति ॥ (*atretihāsamācakṣate | devāpiścārṣṭiṣeṇaḥ śaṃtanuśca kauravyau bhrātarā babhūvatuḥ | sa śaṃtunuḥ kanīyānabhiṣecayāṃcakre | devāpistapaḥ pratipede | tataḥ śaṃtano rājye dvādaśa varṣāṇi devo na vavarṣa | tamūcubrāhmaṇā adharmastvayā carito jyeṣṭhaṃ bhrātaramaṃtarityābhiṣecitaṃ tasmātte devo na varṣatīti | sa śaṃtunurdevāpiṃ śiśikṣa rājyena | tamuvāca devāpiḥ purohitaste 'sāni yājayāni ca tveti ‖*) (Ni. 9-11)

In former times, in the Kuru line, a king named Ṛṣṭiṣeṇa had two sons, Devāpi and Śantanu. Of these, Devāpi was the elder and Śantanu the younger. After their father Ṛṣṭiṣeṇa died, his elder son Devāpi wished to become king. But his younger brother Śantanu, giving him no opportunity, desired to be the ruler of the kingdom himself, drove out his elder brother Devāpi and began to rule the kingdom himself. The elder brother Devāpi, distressed, went to the forest and passed his time performing austerities. While matters stood so, no rain fell in Śantanu's country for twelve years and a great famine arose. Then King Śantanu considered what should be done about it and asked the advice of the brāhmaṇas. The brāhmaṇas said to the king: "According to the Dharmaśāstra, the king's eldest son alone is entitled to the kingdom. Therefore the rule of the kingdom should have been exercised by your elder brother Devāpi. But you gave him no opportunity, drove him out and seized the kingdom. Since you are thus practising unrighteousness, the god of rain does not send rain in your kingdom." King Śantanu repented of the wrong he had done, summoned his elder brother Devāpi, who was performing austerities in the forest, and handed the kingdom over to him. But Devāpi said that he did not want the kingdom, that he would remain in the forest performing austerities and follow the path of withdrawal (निवृत्ति (*nivṛtti*)); and that, for the removal of the drought that had arisen in the kingdom, Śantanu should perform a sacrifice, in which he himself would take the office of priest and help bring about the rain. The king agreed, and Devāpi took the part of the priest called hotṛ in his sacrifice.

### Printed p. 98

Brihaspati became the Brahman priest in that sacrifice, performed for the sake of the fall of rain. The Hotṛ priest Devāpi addressed him, the Brahman Bṛhaspati: "O Bṛhaspati, you have great influence over the minds of the gods. Therefore teach me the mantras that win them over." Thus he prayed. Bṛhaspati agreed, and taught Devāpi, the Hotṛ, mantras containing the prayers to the gods needed for bringing rain. When offerings were made to the gods with those mantras, rain fell abundantly, it is said. Therefore this sūkta is also called the Vṛṣṭikāma-sūkta. With some variations this matter is also told in the Purāṇic works: Viṣṇu Purāṇa 4-20-7, the Mahābhārata Ādiparva, Bhāgavata Purāṇa 9-22-14 to 17, and others. In the work Bṛhaddevatā too the sage Śaunaka has said as follows about this sūkta:

आर्ष्विषेणस्तु देवापिः कौरव्यश्चैव शंतनुः (*ārṣviṣeṇastu devāpiḥ kauravyaścaiva śaṃtanuḥ*) …
(155) In former times Ṛṣṭiṣeṇa of the Kuru line had a son named Devāpi. Among the kings of the Kuru line there was a prince named Śantanu. These two princes of the Kuru line were brothers. Of them,

(156) Devāpi was the elder and Śantanu the younger. Devāpi, the son of Ṛṣṭiṣeṇa, had a skin disease.

(157) After his father died, the people of that country entrusted the kingdom to Devāpi. Having reflected for a moment, Devāpi spoke to his subjects thus.

Here ends the seventh chapter.

The eighth chapter

### Printed p. 99

(1) "O subjects, since I am afflicted with a skin disease, I am not fit to rule the kingdom. Therefore let Śantanu be your king." So he said. Saying "So be it," the subjects consecrated Śantanu as king.

(2) After Śantanu of the Kuru line was consecrated, Devāpi went to the forest to practise austerity. Then, in Śantanu's kingdom, Parjanya did not send rain for twelve years.

(3) Therefore King Śantanu set out with his subjects and came to the forest where Devāpi was performing austerity. He showed repentance for having become king by transgressing dharma (that is, by becoming king himself without giving the kingdom to his elder brother), and pacified him in many ways.

(4) Then Śantanu, together with his subjects, offered the kingdom to his elder brother Devāpi. Seeing Śantanu standing before him with folded hands, humble, Devāpi spoke thus.

(5) "O king, since I am suffering from a skin disease, I am not fit to rule. But so that your kingdom may have plenty through the fall of rain, I myself will stand by (taking up the office of priest) and have you perform the sacrifice." So he said.

(6) Then King Śantanu agreed, saying "So be it," and requested Devāpi to take up the office of priest in the sacrifice he was to perform, and made him chief priest. Devāpi, having taken the priestly office, duly carried out the rites to be performed for causing rain.

### Printed p. 100

(7) Afterwards Devāpi worshipped Bṛhaspati with three Ṛks (1-3) of the 98th sūkta beginning बृहस्पते प्रति (*bṛhaspate prati*). In the second Ṛk of this sūkta Jātavedas Agni, addressing Devāpi, said—

(8) "O Devāpi, I place in your mouth a very brilliant speech (a mantra of exceeding power) — that is, I shall teach it to you. With it praise the gods." Bṛhaspati, pleased, granted Devāpi mantras effective for bringing rain. By the power of those mantras,

(9) Devāpi prayed to the gods with four Ṛks (Ṛks 4-7 of the 98th sūkta) for the fall of rain, and in the remaining later Ṛks of the sūkta (8-12) he praised Agni, it is said. Wilson, the Western scholar, writes as follows—

##### English translation
The origin of this Sukta is a legend quoted by Sayana from the Nirukata (II. 10.) Devapi and Shantanu were sons of Rishtishena of the Kuru family : the younger Shantanu became Raja, Devapi having made choice of a life of penance. In Shantanu's kingdom there was no rain for twelve years—the consequence, according to the Brahmans who were consulted, of Shantanu's having improperly assumed the government while his elder brother, the more legitimate Raja, was living. He was desirous of resigning his authority, but Devapi declined to accept the throne, saying " Let me be your Purohita, and conduct your religious rites." The story is told in the Vishnu Purana, iv. 20. 7; Wilson, pp. 457, 458; in the Mahabharata (Adi Parva, 3750); in Bhagavata Purana, IX. 22. 14—17; and other Puranas, sometimes with the addition that Devapi had disqualified himself from ascending the throne by the adaption of doctrines contrary to the Vedas, and consequently Shantanu remained Raja. According to the Bhagavata and

### Printed p. 101

Matsya Puranas, Devapi is still alive at a place called Kalapagrama. This Sukta is remarkable as representing one of two brothers, both of the Kshatriya caste, becoming the Purohita, or family priest, and Hotri or sacrificing priest, of the other who is the Raja. (See Muir's Sanskrit Texts, vol, ii. pp. 143-150. Muir gives a translation of this hymn, verses 1—9)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> बृहस्पते प्रतीति द्वादशर्चमष्टमं सूक्तं त्रैष्टुभं बृहस्पतिमित्रादिसर्वदेवताकं । ऋष्टिषेणपुत्रो देवापिर्नामर्षः । तथा चानुक्रांतं । बृहस्पते द्वादशार्श्विषेणो देवापिर्वृष्टिकामो देवांस्तुष्टाव ॥ गतः सूक्तविनियोगः ॥ अस्य
> सूक्तस्याख्यानं निरुक्तकारः प्रदर्शयति । देवापिश्चार्ष्विषेणः शंतनुश्च कौरव्यौ
> भ्रातरौ बभूवतुः । स शंतनुः कनीयानभिषेचयांचक्रे देवापिस्तपः प्रतिपेदे ।
> ततः शंतनो राज्ये द्वादश वर्षाणि देवो न ववर्ष । तमूचुब्राह्मणा अधर्मस्त्वया चरितो ज्येष्ठं भ्रातरमंतरित्याभिषेचितं तस्मात्ते देवो न वर्षतीति । स
> शंतनुर्देवापिं शिशिक्ष राज्येन । तमुवाच देवापिः पुरोहितस्तेऽसानि याजयानि च
> त्वेति । तस्यैतद्वर्षकामसूक्तं । नि. २-१० । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta बृहस्पते प्रति (*bṛhaspate prati*) is the eighth sūkta, of twelve Ṛks, in Triṣṭubh metre, with Bṛhaspati, Mitra and all the gods as deity. Its seer is Devāpi, son of Ṛṣṭiṣeṇa. The Anukramaṇī is quoted: बृहस्पते द्वादशार्श्विषेणो देवापिर्वृष्टिकामो देवांस्तुष्टाव (*bṛhaspate dvādaśārśviṣeṇo devāpirvṛṣṭikāmo devāṃstuṣṭāva*). The use of the sūkta has been stated. The author of the Nirukta shows the story: Devāpi and Śantanu, of the Kuru line, were brothers; Śantanu, the younger, had himself consecrated, and Devāpi took to austerity. Then in Śantanu's kingdom the god did not rain for twelve years. The Brāhmaṇas told him: "You have done unrighteousness, in having yourself consecrated passing over your elder brother; therefore the god does not rain for you." Śantanu pressed the kingdom on Devāpi. Devāpi said: "I shall be your priest and perform the sacrifice for you." This is his rain-seeking sūkta (Ni. 2-10).

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta बृहस्पते प्रति (*bṛhaspate prati*) is the eighth sūkta in the eighth anuvāka; it has twelve Ṛks. Its seer is Devāpi, son of Ṛṣṭiṣeṇa; its deities are Bṛhaspati, Mitra and others. The Anukramaṇī says as quoted above. The use of the sūkta was stated earlier. Regarding this sūkta, Yāska, the author of the Nirukta, has told a story. Ṛṣṭiṣeṇa, a king of the Kuru line, had two sons, Devāpi and Śantanu. Devāpi was the elder, Śantanu the younger. But the younger Śantanu was consecrated and began to rule. The elder Devāpi went to the forest and practised austerity. In Śantanu's kingdom Parjanya sent no rain for twelve years. The Brāhmaṇas, understanding the cause, said to King Śantanu: "O king, without giving the kingdom to your elder brother Devāpi, you, the younger, rule as a consecrated king. This is unrighteousness. Because such unrighteousness has been done by you, there was no rain in your kingdom." Then Śantanu went to Devāpi in the forest and begged him to accept the kingdom. But Devāpi, not agreeing, said: "I do not want the kingdom. But so that rain may fall in your kingdom, I myself shall take the office of priest and have you perform the sacrifice." Therefore this sūkta came to be called the Varṣakāma-sūkta (Ni. 2-10).

### Printed p. 102

Maṇḍala 10; Anuvāka 8; Sūkta 98; Aṣṭaka 8; Adhyāya 5; Varga 12, 13. Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 12.
Ṛṣi: Devāpi Ārṣṭiṣeṇa. Devatā: the Devas. Chandas: Triṣṭubh.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> बृह॑स्पते॒ प्रति॑ मे दे॒वता॑मिहि मि॒त्रो वा॒ यद्वरु॑णो॒ वासि॑  ॒
> पूषा ।  
> आ॒दि॒त्यैर्वा॒ यद्वसु॑भिर्म॒रुत्वा॒न्न प॒र्जन्यं॒ शंत॑नवे  
> वृषाय ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> बृहस्पते । प्रति । मे । देवतां । इहि । मित्रः । वा । यत् । वरुणः । वा । असि । पूषा ।  
> आदित्यैः । वा । यत् । वसुभिः । मरुत्वान् । सः । पर्जन्यं । शंततनवे । वृषय ॥१॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> तत्र ब्रह्मत्वे प्रवृत्तो बृहस्पतिमनुधावति । हे बृहस्पते मे मम वृष्ट्यर्थं
> प्रति देवतां प्रतीहि । प्रतिगच्छ । यष्टव्या देवताः प्रतिगच्छ । यदि त्वं मित्रो वासि
> अथवा वरुणोऽसि यद्वा पूषासि अथवादित्यैर्द्वादशादित्यैररुणादिभिर्वसुभिर्वासकैरष्टवसुभिर्धरध्रुवादिभिः सह मरुत्वान् । मरुतो देवाः । तद्वानसि । स त्वं
> पर्जन्यं तर्पयितारं मेघं शंतनने राज्ञॆ वृषाय ! वर्षय ॥ छंदसि
> शायजपीति व्यत्ययेन शपोऽपि शायजादेशः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Engaged there in the office of Brahman, Devāpi runs after Bṛhaspati. "O Bṛhaspati, for my rain, go to the deities, go to the gods to whom offering is to be made, whether you are Mitra, or Varuṇa, or Pūṣan, or accompanied by the twelve Ādityas such as Aruṇa, and by the eight Vasus such as Dhara and Dhruva, and so Marutvān, possessed of the Maruts, who are gods. Such as you are, make Parjanya, the satisfying cloud, rain for King Śantanu." (The remainder concerns the verb form.)

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- बृहस्पते (*bṛhaspate*)—O Bṛhaspati
- मे (*me*)—for me, for the sake of rain
- देवतां प्रति इहि (*devatāṃ prati ihi*)—go to the gods and pray
- यत् (*yat*)—if
- मित्रः (*mitraḥ*)—in the form of Mitra
- असि (*asi*)—you are
- वरुणः (*varuṇaḥ*)—even in the form of Varuṇa
- पूषा वा असि (*pūṣā vā asi*)—even if you are Pūṣan
- (or)

### Printed p. 103

- आदित्यैः (*ādityaiḥ*)—with the Ādityas
- वसुभिः (*vasubhiḥ*)—with the Vasus, joined
- मरुत्वान् (*marutvān*)—even if endowed with the Maruts
- सः (*saḥ*)—such a you
- शंतनवे (*śaṃtanave*)—for the king named Śantanu
- पर्जन्यं (*parjanyaṃ*)—Parjanya, the god, impelling
- वृषय (*vṛṣaya*)—cause to shower rain

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Bṛhaspati, go to the gods and pray that rain be provided for me. Even if you are in the form of Mitra, or Varuṇa, or Pūṣan, or are joined with the Ādityas and with the Vasus and are endowed with the Maruts, impel Parjanya for the king named Śantanu and cause rain to fall.

##### English translation
Repair, Brihaspathi, on my behalf to the gods, whether you are Mitra, or Varuna, or Pushan, or are associated with the Adityas, the Vasus, or the Maruts—do you cause Parjanya to send down rain for Shantanu.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ दे॒वो दू॒तो अ॑जि॒रश्चि॑कि॒त्वान्वद्दे॑वापे अ॒भि माम॑गच्छत् ।  
> प्र॒ती॒ची॒नः प्रति॒ मामा न॑वृत्स्व॒ दधा॑मि ते द्यु॒मतीं॒ वाच॑मा॒सन् ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । देवः । दूतः । अजिरः । चिकित्वान् । त्वत् । देव; आपे । अभि । मां । अगच्छत् ।  
> प्रतीचीनः । प्रति । मां । आ । ववृत्स्व । दधामि । ते । द्युमतीं । वाचं । आसन् ॥२॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> देव कश्चिद्दूतोऽजिरो गमनशीलश्चि कित्वांश्चेतनावान् हे देवापे त्वत्
> त्वत्तः सकाशात्त्वया प्रेषितः सन् मामभ्यागच्छत् । अभ्यागच्छतु । हे बृहस्पते
> प्रतीचीनोऽस्मदभिमुखो मां प्रत्या ववृत्स्य । मां प्रत्यागच्छ । ते तुभ्यं त्वदर्थं
> द्युमतीं दीप्तियुक्तां वाचं स्तुतिरूपां दधाम्यासन्नास्मेऽस्मदीये ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

A certain god, a messenger, swift (going by nature) and intelligent, sent by you from your presence, O Devāpi, should come to me. O Bṛhaspati, turn towards us, facing us; come to me. For your sake I place in our mouth a radiant speech, in the form of praise.

### Printed p. 104

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- देवापे (*devāpe*)—O Devāpi
- अजिरः (*ajiraḥ*)—swift-going
- चिकित्वान् (*cikitvān*)—wise
- देवः दूतः (*devaḥ dūtaḥ*)—some divine messenger
- त्वत् (*tvat*)—sent by you
- मां (*māṃ*)—to me
- अभ्यागच्छत् (*abhyāgacchat*)—let him come. (O Bṛhaspati)
- प्रतीचीनः (*pratīcīnaḥ*)—turning towards me
- मां प्रति (*māṃ prati*)—to me
- आ ववृत्स्य (*ā vavṛtsya*)—turn back and come
- ते (*te*)—addressing you
- द्युमतीं (*dyumatīṃ*)—radiant
- वाचं (*vācaṃ*)—praise
- आसन् (*āsan*)—in the mouth
- दधामि (*dadhāmi*)—I hold

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Devāpi, let some divine messenger, swift-going and wise, sent by you, come to me. O Bṛhaspati, turn towards me and come back to me; addressing you, I recite with my mouth a radiant praise.

##### English translation
Let a divine messenger quick and intelligent sent, Devapi, by you come to me. Come, Brihaspati, to me, turning towards me; I have in my mouth a brilliant eulogium for you.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒स्मे धे॑हि द्यु॒मतीं॒ वाच॑मा॒सन्बृह॑स्पते अनमी॒वामि॑षि॒रां।  
> यया॑ वृ॒ष्टिं शंत॑नवे॒ वना॑व दि॒वो द्र॒प्सो मधु॑मा॒ आ  ॑
> विवेश ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अस्मे इति । धेहि । द्युमतीं । वाचं । आसन् । बृहस्पते । अनमीवां । इषिरां ।  
> यया । वृष्टिं । शंतनवे । वनव । दिवः । द्रप्सः । मधुमान् । आ । विवेश ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे बृहस्पते त्वमस्मे अस्मासु द्युमतीं दीप्तिमतीं वाचं स्तोत्रात्मिकामासन्नास्येऽस्मदीये धेहि । स्थापय । कीदृशीं वाचं । अनमीवाममीवानारहितां ।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Bṛhaspati, you place in us, in our mouth, a radiant speech consisting of praise. What kind of speech? One that is अनमीवां (*anamīvāṃ*), free from *amīvā*.

### Printed p. 105

> वाचोऽमीना नाम गद्गदादिदोषः । तथेषिरां गमनशीलां । यया वाचा
> स्तुत्यात्मिकया देवानिष्ट्वा शंतनवे वृष्टिकामाय वनाव संभजेवहि वृष्टिं त्वं
> चाहं च दिवो द्युलोकात् । त्वयाधिष्ठितो द्रप्स उदकस्यंदो मधुमान् माधुर्योपेत आ विवेश । अविशति वाचमिति समन्वयः !!

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

*amīvā* here is a fault of speech such as stammering. Likewise the speech is इषिरां (*iṣirāṃ*), "going swiftly". By the speech of praise with which, having worshipped the gods, you and I may share वनाव (*vanāva*) rain from heaven for Śantanu, who desires rain, the honeyed stream of water, presided over by you and endowed with sweetness, has entered. The construction is: it enters the speech.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- बृहस्पते (*bṛhaspate*)—O Bṛhaspati
- यया (*yayā*)—by which praise
- शंतनवे (*śaṃtanave*)—for Śantanu
- वृष्टिं (*vṛṣṭiṃ*)—rain
- वनाव (*vanāva*)—we desire (as the result of which)
- मधुमान् (*madhumān*)—sweet
- द्रप्सः (*drapsaḥ*)—the stream of water
- दिवः (*divaḥ*)—flowing from the heavenly world
- आ विवेश (*ā viveśa*)—enters (into us), such
- अनमीवां (*anamīvāṃ*)—faultless
- इषिरां (*iṣirāṃ*)—going swiftly among the gods
- द्युमतीं (*dyumatīṃ*)—radiant
- वाचं (*vācaṃ*)—praise
- अस्मे (*asme*)—our
- आसन् (*āsan*)—in the mouth
- धेहि (*dhehi*)—place

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Bṛhaspati, place in our mouth (teach us) such a praise, faultless, swift in going to the gods and radiant, by which we desire rain for Śantanu, as the result of which a sweet stream of water flows from the heavenly world and enters into us.

##### English translation
Put, Brihaspati, in my mouth a brilliant eulogium, prompt and free from defect, by which we two may obtain rain from heaven for Shantanu' the sweet drop (sent by you) has entered (our voice).

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ नो॑ द्र॒प्सा मधु॑मंतो विशं॒त्विंद्र॑ दे॒ह्यधि॑रथं स॒हस्रं॑ ।  
> नि षी॑द हो॒त्रमृ॑तु॒था य॑जस्व दे॒वान्दे॑वापे ह॒विषा॑ सपर्य ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । नः । द्रप्साः । मधुमंतः । विशंतु । इंद्र । देहि । अधिरथं । सहस्रं ।  
> नि । सीद । होत्रं । ऋतुथा । यजस्व । देवान् । देवआपे । हविषा । सपर्य ॥४॥

### Printed p. 106

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> नोऽस्मान्द्रप्सा वृष्टिसंस्कृया मधुमंतो माधुर्योपेता आ विशंतु ।
> हे इंद्र परमेश्वर बृहस्पते अधिरथं रथस्याध्युपरि वर्तमानं सहस्रं सहस्रसंख्याकं
> धनं देहि । धेहि । यद्वा । रथमधिकं यस्य सहस्रस्य तादृशं गोसहस्रं । हे
> देवापे नि षीद होत्रं । आर्त्विज्ये निषीद । निषण्णश्च त्वमृतुथा काले काले यजस्व
> यष्टव्यान्देवान् स्तुत्या हविषा च सपर्य । परिचर ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

May the sweet streams, formed by rain, enter us. O Indra, that is, the Lord Bṛhaspati, give a thousand, a thousand-fold wealth, lying upon the chariot. Or else: a thousand cows, a chariot-load more than the thousand. O Devāpi, sit down at the Hotṛ's office, that is, sit down for the Ṛtvij's work. Seated, sacrifice at the proper season to the gods to be worshipped, and serve them with praise and with oblation.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- मधुमंतः (*madhumaṃtaḥ*)—endowed with sweetness
- द्रप्साः (*drapsāḥ*)—streams of water (drops)
- नः (*naḥ*)—us
- आ विशंतु (*ā viśaṃtu*)—let them enter
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Bṛhaspati, the Lord
- अधिरथं (*adhirathaṃ*)—lying upon the chariot
- सहस्रं (*sahasraṃ*)—wealth of a thousand
- धेहि (*dhehi*)—place in us
- देवापे (*devāpe*)—O Devāpi
- होत्रं (*hotraṃ*)—in the Hotṛ's place
- नि षीद (*ni ṣīda*)—sit down
- ऋतुथा (*ṛtuthā*)—according to the respective seasons
- देवान् (*devān*)—the gods
- यजस्व (*yajasva*)—worship
- हविषा (*haviṣā*)—with oblations
- सपर्य (*saparya*)—serve

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Let streams of water endowed with sweetness come and enter us. O Bṛhaspati, the Lord, place in us the thousand-fold wealth lying upon the chariot. O Devāsi **NOTE**, sit down in the Hotṛ's place. Worship the gods according to the respective seasons. Serve them with oblations.

##### English translation
May the sweet drops of rain fall upon us : grant us, Indra, a thousand chariot-loads. Sit down, Devapi, to your duties as Hotri ; in due season sacrifice to the gods, worship them with oblations.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ॒र्ष्टि॒षे॒णो हो॒त्रमृषि॑र्नि॒षीद॑न्दे॒वापि॑र्देवसुम॒तिं चि॑कि॒त्वान् ।  
> स उत्त॑रस्मा॒दध॑रं समु॒द्रम॒पो दि॒व्या अ॑सृद्व॒र्ष्या॑ अ॒भि ॥ ५ ॥

### Printed p. 107

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आर्ष्टिषेणः । होत्रं । ऋषिः । निसीदन् । देवआपिः । देवसुमतिं । चिकित्वान् ।  
> सः । उत्तरस्मात् । अधरं । समुद्रं । अपः । दिव्याः । असृजत् । वर्ष्याः । अभि ॥५॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> आर्ष्टिषेण ऋष्टिषेणस्य पुत्रो देवापिर्यषिर्देवसुमतिं देवानां
> कल्याणीं मतिं स्तुतिं चिकित्वाञ्ज्ञानन् होत्रं होतृकर्म कर्तुं निषीदन् निषण्णो
> भवति । स उत्तरस्मादुपरि वर्तमानादंतरिक्षाख्यात्समुद्रादधरमधो वर्तमानं
> पार्थिवं समुद्रमभि दिव्या दिवि भवा वर्ष्या वर्षभवा अपोऽसृजत् । सृजतु ।
> अत्रार्ष्टिषेण ऋष्टिषेणस्य पुत्र इत्यादि निरुक्तं । २-११ । द्रष्टव्यं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Ārṣṭiṣeṇa, the son of Ṛṣṭiṣeṇa, the seer Devāpi, knowing देवसुमतिं (*devasumatiṃ*), the gracious thought, the praise, of the gods, has sat down to perform the Hotṛ's work. May he, from the upper ocean, called the atmosphere, send down to the lower ocean, the terrestrial one, the waters born in heaven, born of rain. Here the Nirukta (2-11), beginning with "Ārṣṭiṣeṇa, the son of Ṛṣṭiṣeṇa", is to be consulted.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- आर्ष्टिषेणः (*ārṣṭiṣeṇaḥ*)—the son of Ṛṣṭiṣeṇa
- देवापिः (*devāpiḥ*)—named Devāpi
- ऋषिः (*ṛṣiḥ*)—the seer
- देवसुमतिं (*devasumatiṃ*)—the praise that brings about the favour of the gods
- चिकित्वान् (*cikitvān*)—having known
- होत्रं (*hotraṃ*)—in the Hotṛ's place (to praise)
- निषीदन् (*niṣīdan*)—is seated
- सः (*saḥ*)—he
- उत्तरस्मात् (*uttarasmāt*)—from the ocean in the atmosphere
- अधरं समुद्रं अभि (*adharaṃ samudraṃ abhi*)—to the ocean on the earth
- दिव्याः (*divyāḥ*)—born of the heavenly world
- वर्ष्याः (*varṣyāḥ*)—born of rain
- अपः (*apaḥ*)—waters
- असृजत् (*asṛjat*)—let him pour down

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The seer named Devāpi, the son of Ṛṣṭiṣeṇa **NOTE**, knowing the praise that brings about the favour of the gods, is seated in the Hotṛ's place to praise. May he pour down, from the ocean in the atmosphere, that is, from the cloud-region, to the ocean on the earth, the waters born of the heavenly world and of rain **NOTE**.

##### English translation
The Rishi Devapi, the son of Rishtishena, knowing how to propitate the gods has sat down to his functions as Hotri. He has brought down from the upper to the lower ocean the rain waters of heaven.

### Printed p. 108

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

For this Ṛk Yāska has given a Nirukta and the derivation of the forms of some words: आर्ष्टिषेणो होत्रमृषिर्निषीदन्नेवापिर्देवसुमतिं चिकित्वान् । (*ārṣṭiṣeṇo hotramṛṣirniṣīdannevāpirdevasumatiṃ cikitvān |*) स उत्तरस्मा दधरं समुद्रमपो दिव्या अस्यजद्वर्ष्या अभि ॥ (*sa uttarasmā dadharaṃ samudramapo divyā asyajadvarṣyā abhi ‖*) आर्ष्टिषेण ऋष्टिषेणस्य पुत्र इषितसेनस्येति वा । (*ārṣṭiṣeṇa ṛṣṭiṣeṇasya putra iṣitasenasyeti vā |*) सेना सेश्वरा समानगतिर्वा । (*senā seśvarā samānagatirvā |*) पुत्रः पुरु त्रायते निपरणाद्वा पुन्नरकं ततस्तायत इति वा । (*putraḥ puru trāyate niparaṇādvā punnarakaṃ tatastāyata iti vā |*) होतृमृषिर्निषीदन् । (*hotṛmṛṣirniṣīdan |*) ऋषिर्दर्शनात् स्तोमान् ददर्शेत्यौपमन्यवः तद्यदेनांस्तपस्यमानान् ब्रह्म स्वयंभ्यभ्यानर्षत्त ऋषयोऽभवंस्तदृषीणामृषित्वमिति विज्ञायते । द (*ṛṣirdarśanāt stomān dadarśetyaupamanyavaḥ tadyadenāṃstapasyamānān brahma svayaṃbhyabhyānarṣatta ṛṣayo 'bhavaṃstadṛṣīṇāmṛṣitvamiti vijñāyate | da*) देवापिर्देवानामाप्ता स्तुत्या च प्रदानेन च । (*devāpirdevānāmāptā stutyā ca pradānena ca |*) देवसुमतिं देवानां कल्याणीं मतिं । (*devasumatiṃ devānāṃ kalyāṇīṃ matiṃ |*) चिकित्ांश्चेतनावान् । (*cikitāṃścetanāvān |*) स उत्तरस्मादधरं समुद्रं उत्तर उद्धततरो भवति। (*sa uttarasmādadharaṃ samudraṃ uttara uddhatataro bhavati|*) अधरोऽधोरः । (*adharo 'dhoraḥ |*) अधो न धावतीतूर्ध्वगतिः प्रतिषिद्दा॥ (*adho na dhāvatītūrdhvagatiḥ pratiṣiddā‖*) (Ni. 2-11)

Ārṣṭiṣeṇa: the son of one named Ṛṣṭiṣeṇa is called Ārṣṭiṣeṇa. Ṛṣṭiṣeṇa: ṛṣṭi is a special weapon like a spear. One who is accompanied by an army having many such spears is called Ṛṣṭiṣeṇa. Or इषितसेनो वा (*iṣitaseno vā*): because he constantly sends forth his armies against enemies, so that they always rush upon the foe, he has the name Iṣitasena or Ṛṣṭiṣeṇa.

सेना सेश्वरा समानगतिर्वा (*senā seśvarā samānagatirvā*): the meaning of the word senā is this: ina means īśvara, master. Senā is a host of warriors together with a master or lord; that is, a host of warriors with a commander is called senā. Or समानगतिर्वा (*samānagatirvā*): those who go with one kind of motion (marching in formation); that is, when the warriors of an army march, a host of warriors who walk evenly in rows, four or five to a line, or who go with the one aim of winning victory, is called senā.

पुत्रः पुरु त्रायते निपरणाद्वा । पुन्नरकं तशस्त्रायत इति वा (*putraḥ puru trāyate niparaṇādvā | punnarakaṃ taśastrāyata iti vā*) **NOTE**—how did the son get the name putra? Because he offers piṇḍa and the like to the forefathers (at the śrāddha) he is called putra; or put is the name of a hell. A son, by the piṇḍa-offering and other rites he performs for his forefathers and by other good deeds, त्रायते (*trāyate*) protects his forefathers so that they do not fall into that hell called put; therefore he is called putra. By the good deeds the son performs, the father's attaining of hells such as put is averted and a good state is attained. Therefore he who protects the father from the hell put is called putra.

### Printed p. 109
होत्रमृषिर्निषीदन् (*hotramṛṣirniṣīdan*): this Devāpi, in order to have a rain-giving sacrifice performed for Śantanu, sits on the sacrificial altar as Hotṛ, that is, as Ṛtvij, to do the Hotṛ's work; this is the meaning.

ऋषिर्दर्शनात् स्तोमान् ददर्शेत्यौपमन्यवः तद्यदेनांस्तपस्यमानान् ब्रह्म स्वयंभ्वभ्यानर्षत्त ऋषयोऽभवस्तदृषीणामृषित्वमिति विज्ञायते (*ṛṣirdarśanāt stomān dadarśetyaupamanyavaḥ tadyadenāṃstapasyamānān brahma svayaṃbhvabhyānarṣatta ṛṣayo 'bhavastadṛṣīṇāmṛṣitvamiti vijñāyate*)—the derivation and formation of the word ṛṣi is given. A ṛṣi is one who can see, that is, one who by his supersensory knowledge can see and know past and future things that are not perceptible to ordinary men; he is called ṛṣi. The view of the teacher Aupamanyava is that the name ṛṣi came because he sees stomas or stotras (mantras) by his supersensory knowledge. On this matter Yāska has cited the Brāhmaṇa (Śruti) sentence तद्यदेनांस्तपस्यमानान् ब्रह्म स्वयंभ्वभ्यावर्षत्त ऋषयोऽभवस्तदृषीणामृषित्वमिति विज्ञायते (*tadyadenāṃstapasyamānān brahma svayaṃbhvabhyāvarṣatta ṛṣayo 'bhavastadṛṣīṇāmṛṣitvamiti vijñāyate*); it is understood from the Śruti sentence that to the seers who were practising austerity the self-existent Brahman itself, that is, the Vedic mantra, not of human origin, came near and became perceptible. Even though the seers do not study their mantras, they realise them directly by the power of their austerity. Therefore this derivation has arisen for the word ṛṣi.

देवापिर्देवानामाप्त्या स्तुत्या च प्रदानेन च (*devāpirdevānāmāptyā stutyā ca pradānena ca*)—Devāpi means: देवानामाप्त्या स हि स्तुतिभिः हविःसंप्रदाने च देवानाप्नोति । (*devānāmāptyā sa hi stutibhiḥ haviḥsaṃpradāne ca devānāpnoti |*) Because he obtains the gods (āptyā) by stotras and the like and by offering oblations and the like, that is, because he brings them, he is called Devāpi.

देवसुमतिं देवानां कल्याणीं मतिं (*devasumatiṃ devānāṃ kalyāṇīṃ matiṃ*)—the excellent or auspicious thought of the gods; one who knows how to acquire the thought of the gods that consists in their favour. चिकित्वान्—चेतनावान् । (*cikitvān—cetanāvān |*) one who knows.

स उत्तरस्मादधरं समुद्रं । उत्तर उद्धततरो भवति । अधरोऽधोरः । अधो न धावतीतूर्ध्वगतिः प्रतिषिद्दा ॥ (*sa uttarasmādadharaṃ samudraṃ | uttara uddhatataro bhavati | adharo 'dhoraḥ | adho na dhāvatītūrdhvagatiḥ pratiṣiddā ‖*) From the upper ocean to the lower ocean; since the word samudra also means atmosphere, the meaning is: from the atmosphere above to the ocean below. When rain falls, the waters in the clouds of the atmosphere above fall upon the earth below and

### Printed p. 110
They flow as rivers and finally reach the sea. Therefore the rain-waters are described as going from the upper ocean (the clouds standing in the atmosphere) and falling into the lower ocean. Uttara means "raised higher", that which is above, still higher up; adhara means "अधोरः (*adhoraḥ*)", that which is below, down below. Since for the water that is below there is no possibility of going upward again, it is called adhoraḥ or adharaḥ (the lower).

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒स्मिन्त्स॑मु॒द्रे अध्युत्त॑रस्मि॒न्नापो॑ दे॒वेभि॒र्निवृ॑ता अतिष्ठन् ।  
> ता अ॑द्रवन्नार्ष्टिषे॒णेन॑ सृ॒ष्टा दे॒वापि॑ना॒ प्रेषि॑ता मृ॒क्षिणी॑षु ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अस्मिन् । समुद्रे । अधि । उत्तरस्मिन् । आपः । देवेभिः । निवृताः । अतिष्ठन् ।  
> शाः । अद्रवन् । आर्ष्टिषेणेन । सृष्टाः । देवआपिना । प्रइषिताः । मृक्षिणेषु ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अस्मिन्स्पार्थिवे समुद्रे पूरणीये सति । अधीति सप्तम्यर्थानुवादी । उत्तरस्मिन्समुद्रेऽंतरिक्षाख्य आप उदकानि देवेभिर्द्योतमानैर्निवृता निरुद्धा अतिष्ठन्
> ता आप आर्ष्टिषेणेनर्ष्टिषेणस्य पुत्रेण देवापिना सृष्टाः प्रकर्षेणेच्छां
> प्राप्ताः कांक्षिता मृक्षिणेषु मृष्टवतीषु परिमृष्टासु स्थलीष्वद्रवन् । स्रवंति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

While this earthly ocean is to be filled, in the other, upper ocean called the atmosphere, the waters were held back by the shining gods and stood still. Those waters, sent forth by Devapi, the son of Ṛṣṭiṣeṇa, having been greatly desired and longed for, ran onto the wiped-clean, well-prepared (fertile) plains. अधि (*adhi*) merely restates the locative sense.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अस्मिन् समुद्रे अधि (*asmin samudre adhi*)—when this earthly ocean is to be filled
- उत्तरस्मिन् (*uttarasmin*)—in the ocean situated in the atmosphere
- आपः (*āpaḥ*)—waters
- देवेभिः (*devebhiḥ*)—by the gods
- निवृताः (*nivṛtāḥ*)—held back
- अतिष्ठन् (*atiṣṭhan*)—stood right there
- आर्ष्टिषेणेन (*ārṣṭiṣeṇena*)—by the son of Ṛṣṭiṣeṇa
- देवापिना (*devāpinā*)—by Devapi
- सृष्टाः (*sṛṣṭāḥ*)—poured out
- प्रेषिताः (*preṣitāḥ*)—sent to the earth
- ताः (*tāḥ*)—those same waters
- मृक्षिणीषु (*mṛkṣiṇīṣu*)—in fertile foothill regions
- अद्रवन् (*adravan*)—flowed and drench the ground

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

When this earthly ocean was to be filled, the waters of the cloud-masses of the atmosphere were held back by the gods and stood there. But those same waters, poured out by Devapi, the son of Ṛṣṭiṣeṇa, and sent to the earth,

### Printed p. 111
flow in fertile foothill regions and drench the ground.

##### English translation
In this upper ocean the waters stood dammed up by the gods; set free by Devapi, the son of Rishthishena, they were sent forth over the plains

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यद्दे॒वापिः॒ शंत॑नवे पु॒रोहि॑तो हो॒त्राय॑ वृ॒तः कृ॒पय॒न्नदी॑धेत् ।  
> दे॒व॒श्रुतं॑ वृष्टि॒वनिं॒ ररा॑णो॒ बृह॒स्पति॒र्वाच॑मस्मा अयच्छत् ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यत् । देवआपिः । शंततनवे । पुरःहितः । होत्राय । वृतः । कृपयन् । अदीधेत् ।  
> देवश्रुतं । वृष्टिवनिं । रराणः । बृहस्पतिः । वाचं । अस्मै । आयच्छत् ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यद्यदा देवापिराष्टिषेणः शंतनवे स्वभ्रात्रे कौरन्याय पुरोहितः सन्
> होत्राय होत्रार्थं वृतः सन् देवश्रुतं । देवा एनं शृण्वंतीति देवश्रुत् ।
> तं तथा वृष्टिवनिं वृष्टियाचिनं बृहस्पतिमदीधेत् अन्वध्यायत् स च रराणो
> रममाणो बृहस्पतिर्देवोऽस्मै देवापये वाचमयच्छत् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

When Devapi Āṛṣṭiṣeṇa, being the purohita of his brother Śantanu of the Kuru line and chosen for the office of hotṛ, meditated on Bṛhaspati, "the one heard by the gods" (the gods hear him), and likewise the one who asks for rain, then Bṛhaspati, delighted, gave this Devapi speech.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यत् (*yat*)—when
- देवापिः (*devāpiḥ*)—Devapi
- शंतनवे (*śaṃtanave*)—for Śantanu
- पुरोहितः (*purohitaḥ*)—as purohita
- होत्राय (*hotrāya*)—for the office of hotṛ
- वृतः (*vṛtaḥ*)—requested
- कृपयन् (*kṛpayan*)—performing his act of praise
- देवश्रुतं (*devaśrutaṃ*)—him who makes the gods hear (his prayer)
- वृष्टिवनिं (*vṛṣṭivaniṃ*)—him who asks rain for mankind; that Bṛhaspati
- अदीधेत् (*adīdhet*)—meditated upon and praised, then
- रराणः (*rarāṇaḥ*)—pleased by this praise
- बृहस्पतिः (*bṛhaspatiḥ*)—Bṛhaspati
- अस्मै (*asmai*)—to this Devapi
- वाचं (*vācaṃ*)—speech in the form of a mantra
- अयच्छत् (*ayacchat*)—gave (taught)

### Printed p. 112

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

When Devapi, purohita of Śantanu, requested to be the hotṛ, performing his hotṛ's work, meditated on and praised Bṛhaspati, who makes the gods hear his prayer and who begs rain for mankind, then Bṛhaspati, pleased by his praise, taught Devapi the rain-bringing mantra.

##### English translation
When Devapi, the Purohita for Shantanu, engaged in his functions of Hotri, compassionating (him), solicited Brihaspati, to whom the gods listen, who begs for rain, (Brihaspati) being pleased gave him a voice.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Yāska has given the Nirukta for this quarter-verse:
यद्देवापिः शंतनवे पुरोहितो होत्राय वृतः कृपयन्नदीधेत् । देवश्रुतं वृष्टिवनिं रराणो बृहस्पतिर्वाचमस्मा अयच्छत् ॥ (*yaddevāpiḥ śaṃtanave purohito hotrāya vṛtaḥ kṛpayannadīdhet | devaśrutaṃ vṛṣṭivaniṃ rarāṇo bṛhaspatirvācamasmā ayacchat ‖*) शंतनुः शं तनोऽस्त्विति वा शमस्यॆ तन्ना अस्त्वति वा । पुरोहितः पुरः एनं दधति । होत्राय वृतः कृपायमाणोऽन्वध्यायत् । देवश्रुतं देवा एनं शृण्वंति । वृष्टिवनिं वृष्टियाचिनं । रराणो रातिरभ्यस्तो बृहस्पति ब्रह्मासीत् सोऽस्मै वाचमयच्छत् बृहदुपव्याख्यातं ॥ (*śaṃtanuḥ śaṃ tano 'stviti vā śamasyè tannā astvati vā | purohitaḥ puraḥ enaṃ dadhati | hotrāya vṛtaḥ kṛpāyamāṇo 'nvadhyāyat | devaśrutaṃ devā enaṃ śṛṇvaṃti | vṛṣṭivaniṃ vṛṣṭiyācinaṃ | rarāṇo rātirabhyasto bṛhaspati brahmāsīt so 'smai vācamayacchat bṛhadupavyākhyātaṃ ‖*) (Ni. 2-12)**NOTE**

शंतनुः शं तनोऽस्त्व वा शमस्मै तन्ना अस्त्विति (*śaṃtanuḥ śaṃ tano 'stva vā śamasmai tannā astviti*)—The reason this king has the name Śantanu: seeing a man suffering from disease, he addressed his body: "O body afflicted by disease, may you be well", and so he got the name Śantanu. Or else: śam means happiness, tanu means body; since he wishes that this body may always have happiness, the name is Śantanu.

पुरोहितः पुर एनं दधति (*purohitaḥ pura enaṃ dadhati*)—In sacrificial and other domestic rites the sacrificers seat him in front of everyone. Because he is placed before all (or before the fire) for carrying out the rite, the name purohita has arisen.

होत्राय वृतः कृपायमाणोऽन्वध्यायत् (*hotrāya vṛtaḥ kṛpāyamāṇo 'nvadhyāyat*)—This Devapi, requested to take the part of the priest called hotṛ in the sacrifice performed for the sake of rain, made the sacrificial fire blaze and praised with stotras and the like.

### Printed p. 113
देवश्रुतं देवा एनं शृण्वंति (*devaśrutaṃ devā enaṃ śṛṇvaṃti*)—The gods hear this Devapi, who recites hymns addressed to the gods; that is, the gods listen to Devapi's praise.

रराणः रातिरभ्यस्तः (*rarāṇaḥ rātirabhyastaḥ*)—The word rarāṇa is formed from the root रा दाने (*rā dāne*). The syllable ra is recited twice (reduplicated). Rarāṇaḥ means giving, giving rain.

बृहस्पतिर्ब्रह्मासीत् सोऽस्मै वाचमयच्छत् बृहदुपव्याख्यातं ॥ (*bṛhaspatirbrahmāsīt so 'smai vācamayacchat bṛhadupavyākhyātaṃ ‖*) In the sacrifice Devapi was performing for rain, Bṛhaspati was the priest called brahman. Therefore he taught Devapi the mantra (vācam) that prays to the gods for rain. Since Devapi was a kṣatriya, he might not know the mantras to be recited by the hotṛ; the meaning is that Bṛhaspati told them to him. The derivation of the word bṛhat has been stated earlier.

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यं त्वा॑ दे॒वापिः॑ शुशुचा॒नो अ॑ग्न आर्ष्टिषे॒णो म॑नु॒ष्यः॑समी॒धे ।  
> विश्वे॑भिर्दे॒वैर॑नुम॒द्यमा॑नः॒ प्र स॒र्जन्य॑मीरया वृष्टि॒मंतं॑ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यं । त्वा । देवआपिः । शुशुचानः । अग्ने । आर्ष्टिषेणः । मनुष्यः । संईधे ।  
> विश्वेभिः । देवैः । अनुमद्यमानः । प्र । पर्जन्यं । ईरय । वृष्टिमंतं ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अग्ने यं त्वा त्वां शशुचानः स्तोत्रेण ज्वलन् मनुष्य आर्ष्टिषेणो
> देवापिः समाधे सम्यग्दीपयति स त्वं विश्वेभिः सर्वैर्देवैरनुमद्यमानोऽनुमाद्यमानः सन् पर्जन्यं मेघं वृष्टिमंतं वर्षणवंतं प्रेरय । गमय ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Agni, you whom the man Devapi Āṛṣṭiṣeṇa, blazing with his praise, kindles well: being gladdened along with all the gods, send forth Parjanya, the cloud, rain-bearing, to go.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अग्ने (*agne*)—O Agni
- यं त्वा (*yaṃ tvā*)—you whom
- शुशुचानः (*śuśucānaḥ*)—he who makes you shine by his praise
- मनुष्यः (*manuṣyaḥ*)—the man
- आर्ष्टिषेणः (*ārṣṭiṣeṇaḥ*)—the son of Ṛṣṭiṣeṇa

### Printed p. 114
- देवापिः (*devāpiḥ*)—Devapi
- समाधे (*samādhe*)—caused to blaze; you who were so
- विश्वेभिः देवैः (*viśvebhiḥ devaiḥ*)—together with all the gods
- अनुमद्यमानः (*anumadyamānaḥ*)—rejoicing
- पर्जन्यं (*parjanyaṃ*)—Parjanya
- वृष्टिमंतं (*vṛṣṭimaṃtaṃ*)—so as to pour rain
- प्र ईरय (*pra īraya*)—impel

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Agni, you whom Devapi, the man, the son of Ṛṣṭiṣeṇa, making you shine by his praise, has kindled: being such, rejoicing together with all the gods, impel Parjanya to pour rain.

##### English translation
Agni, whom the mortal Devapi, the son of Rishtishena, lighting you, has kindled, do you, being propitiated with all the gods, send down the rain laden cloud.

#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> त्वां पूर्व॒ ऋष॑यो गी॒र्भिरा॑य॒न्वाम॑ध्व॒रेषु॑ पुरहूत॒  ॑
> विश्वे ।  
> स॒हस्रा॒ण्यधि॑रथान्य॒स्मे आ नो॑ य॒ज्ञं रो॑हिद॒श्वोप॑ याहि ॥९॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> त्वां । पूर्वे । ऋषयः । गीर्भिरायन्वामध्वरेषु । पुरहूत । विश्वे ।  
> सहस्राणि । अधिरथानि । अस्मे इति । आ । नः । यज्ञं । रोहित्अश्व । उप । याहि॥९॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अग्ने त्वां पूर्व ऋषयो गीर्भिः स्तुतिभिरायन् । आगच्छन् । तथा हे
> पुरुहूत बहुभिराहूताग्ने विश्वे सर्वॆऽपीदानींतना यजमाना अध्वरेषु
> यज्ञेषु स्तुतिभिर्गच्छंतीति शेषः । किंच सहस्राणि सहस्रसंख्याकानि गोयूथान्यधिरथानि रथाधिकान्यस्मे अस्माकं शंतनुना दक्षिणात्वेन संकल्पितानि भवंत्विति शेषः । हे
> रोहिदश्व नो यज्ञमुप याहि । उपागच्छ ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Agni, the ancient seers came to you with hymns of praise. Likewise, O Agni invoked by many (puruhūta), all present-day sacrificers go to you with praises in the sacrifices (this is the word to be supplied). Further, may the thousands of herds of cattle, with chariots in addition, be designated by Śantanu as dakṣiṇā for us (to be supplied). O Agni of red horses, come to our sacrifice.

### Printed p. 115

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O Agni) त्वां (*tvāṃ*)—you
- पूर्व ऋषयः (*pūrva ṛṣayaḥ*)—all the former seers
- गीर्भिः (*gīrbhiḥ*)—with praises
- आयन् (*āyan*)—have resorted to
- पुरुहूत (*puruhūta*)—O Agni, who deserve the invocation of many
- विश्वे (*viśve*)—all present sacrificers too
- अध्वरेषु (*adhvareṣu*)—resort to you alone in the sacrifices
- रोहिदश्व (*rohidaśva*)—O Agni, with golden-coloured horses
- सहस्राणि (*sahasrāṇi*)—thousands of cattle
- अधिरथानि (*adhirathāni*)—riches loaded on the chariot
- अस्मे (*asme*)—give to us
- नः (*naḥ*)—our
- यज्ञं (*yajñaṃ*)—to the sacrifice
- उप याहि (*upa yāhi*)—be pleased to come

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Agni, all the former seers have resorted to you with their praises. O Agni, who deserve the invocation of many, all present sacrificers too resort to you alone in the sacrifices. O Agni of golden-coloured horses, give us thousands of cattle and riches loaded on chariots. Be pleased to come to our sacrifice.

##### English translation
You have ancient sages approached, with hymns; you, O invoked of many, all worshippers (address) at sacrifices; (gives) us thousands of chariot-oads, come to our sacrifice, lord of red horses

#### Ṛk 10

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ए॒तान्य॑ग्ने नव॒तिर्नव॒ त्वे आहु॑ता॒न्यधि॑रथा स॒हस्रा॑ ।  
> तेभि॑र्वर्धस्व त॒न्वः॑ शूर पू॒र्वीर्दि॒वो नो॑ वृ॒ष्टिमि॑षि॒तो  ॑
> रिरीहि ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> एतानि । अग्ने । नवतिः । नव । त्वे इति । आहुतानि । अधिरथा । सहस्रा ।  
> तेभिः । वर्धस्व । तन्वः । शूर । पूर्वीः । दिवः । नः । वृष्टिं । इषितः । रिरीहि ॥ १० ॥

### Printed p. 116
#### Ṛk 10 (continued)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अग्ने गवां नवतिर्नव च तथैतान्यधिरथानि सहस्रा सहस्राणि च रथाधिकानि
> गवां सहस्राणि च त्वे त्वय्याहुतानि त्वयि प्रीणयितव्ये सत्याहुतानि । यद्वा । त्वे
> त्वय्याहुतानि समर्पितानीत्यर्थः । तेभिस्तैः प्रत्तैः पूर्विर्बह्वस्तन्वस्तनूर्युष्मदीया वर्धस्व । वर्धय । नोऽस्मदर्थं दिवो द्युलोकाद्वृष्टिमिषितः प्रार्थितः
> सन् रिरीहि । पूरय ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Agni, these ninety-nine of cattle, and these thousands, exceeding a chariot-load, of cattle, have been offered in you." That is, they have been offered while you are the one to be gratified. Alternatively, "offered in you" means "dedicated to you." "With them", the things given, "make your many bodies, your own forms, grow." "Being solicited on our behalf, fill (send) rain from heaven, the world of light."

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- शूर (*śūra*)—O valiant one
- अग्ने (*agne*)—O Agni
- नवतिः नव एतानि अधिरथा (*navatiḥ nava etāni adhirathā*)—offerings filling ninety-nine chariots
- सहस्रा (*sahasrā*)—animals and the like, a thousand in number
- त्वे (*tve*)—in you
- आहुतानि (*āhutāni*)—have been offered
- तेभिः (*tebhiḥ*)—with them
- पूर्वीः (*pūrvīḥ*)—your manifold
- तन्नः (*tannaḥ*)—bodies
- वर्धस्व (*vardhasva*)—make grow
- इषितः (*iṣitaḥ*)—solicited by us
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- दिवः (*divaḥ*)—from heaven
- वृष्टिं (*vṛṣṭiṃ*)—rain
- रीरिहि (*rīrihi*)—pour down

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O valiant Agni, offerings filling ninety-nine chariots, and animals and the like in thousands, have been offered in you. With them make your manifold bodies grow. Solicited by us, pour down rain for us from heaven.

##### English translation
These ni e y-and-nine thousands of chariot-loads have been offered, Agni, to you ; with them, O hero, nourish yonr many bodies; and thus solicited send us rain from heaven.

#### Ṛk 11

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ए॒तान्य॑ग्ने नव॒तिं स॒हस्रा॒ सं प्र य॑च्छ॒ वृष्ण॒ इंद्रा॑य भा॒गं ।  
> वि॒द्वान्प॒थ ऋ॑तु॒शो दे॑व॒याना॒नपौ॑ला॒नं दि॒वि दे॒वेषु॑  
> धेहि ॥ ११ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> एतानि । अग्ने । नवतिं । सहस्रा । सं । प्र । यच्छ । वृष्णे । इंद्राय । भागं ।  
> विद्वान् । पथः । ऋतुशः । देवयानान् । अपि । औलानं । दिवि । देवेषु । धेहि॥११॥

### Printed p. 117

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अग्ने गवामेतानि नवतिं सहस्रा सहस्राणि च वृष्णे वर्षित्र इंद्राय भागं
> सं प्र यच्छ । तत्प्रीत्यर्थमृत्विग्धो देहि । किंच देवयानान्पथो देवयानान्मा
> र्गान्निद्वांस्तमृतुशः काले काल औलानमसि कुरुकुलजातमसि शांतनवं देवेषु
> मध्यॆ धेहि । निधेहि । स्थापय ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Agni, these ninety (of cattle) and thousands, give together as a share to Indra the showerer (the rain-giver)." For his gratification, "give them to the priests." Further: "Knowing the paths the gods travel, in due season, place the Aulāna, the one born in the Kuru family, the descendant of Śantanu, in heaven among the gods."

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अग्ने (*agne*)—O Agni
- नवतिं (*navatiṃ*)—ninety in number
- सहस्रा (*sahasrā*)—and countless
- एतानि (*etāni*)—these offerings
- वृष्णी (*vṛṣṇī*)—the bestower of desires
- इंद्राय (*iṃdrāya*)—to Indra
- भागं (*bhāgaṃ*)—since it is the share that belongs to him
- सं प्रयच्छ (*saṃ prayaccha*)—offer
- देवयानान् (*devayānān*)—travelled by the gods
- पथः (*pathaḥ*)—the paths
- विद्वान् (*vidvān*)—you who know
- ऋतुतः (*ṛtutaḥ*)—according to the respective seasons
- औलानमपि (*aulānamapi*)—Śantanu, born in the Kuru family
- दिवि (*divi*)—in heaven
- देवेषु (*deveṣu*)—among the gods
- धेहि (*dhehi*)—establish

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Agni, offer to Indra, the bestower of desires, the ninety cows that are his share and the countless offerings. You who know the paths the gods travel, establish Śantanu, born in the Kuru family, in heaven among the gods, according to the respective seasons.

##### English translation
Give, Agni, these nienty thousands to the showerer, Indra, as his share; knowing the paths traversed by the gods in due season place the Aulana (Shantanu) in heaven amongst the gods.

#### Ṛk 12

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अग्ने॒ बाध॑स्व॒ वि मृधो॒ वि दु॒र्गहापामि॑वा॒मप॒ रक्षां॑स  
> सेध ।  
> अ॒स्मात्स॑मु॒द्राद्दृ॑ह॒तो दि॒वो नो॒पां भू॒मान॒मुप॑ न  ॒
> सृजेह ॥ १२ ॥

### Printed p. 118

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अग्ने । बाधस्व । वि । मृधः । वि । दुःगहा । अप । अमीनां । अप । रक्षांसि । सेध ।  
> अस्मात् । समुद्रात् । बृहतः । दिवः । नः । अपां । भूमानं । उप । नः । सृज  
> इह ॥ १२ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अग्ने दुर्गहा दुर्गहाणि दुःखेन गाहितव्यानि शत्रुपुराणि वि बाधस्व ।
> तथामिानां रोगमप सेध । तथा रक्षांस्यप सेध । अपवारय । अस्मात्समुद्रात्
> समुद्रवणसाधनादृहतो महतो दिवो द्युलोकादंतरिक्षाद्वापामुदकानां
> भूमानं बहुभावं वृष्टिसंस्कृयमिहास्मिल्लोक उप सृज । प्रयच्छेत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Agni, destroy the strongholds (durgahā), the enemy cities hard to enter. Likewise drive away disease (amīvā); likewise drive away the Rakṣases, keep them off. From this ocean, from the great heaven, the world of light, or from the sky, send forth here, to this world, the abundance (bhūmāna), the plenty, of the waters, that is, of the rain-formed waters; the meaning is: give."
**NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अग्ने (*agne*)—O Agni
- दुर्गहा (*durgahā*)—the enemy strongholds that others cannot enter
- वि बाधस्व (*vi bādhasva*)—destroy
- अमीवां (*amīvāṃ*)—disease
- अप सेध (*apa sedha*)—destroy
- रक्षांसि (*rakṣāṃsi*)—the Rākṣasas
- अप (सेध) (*apa (sedha)*)—drive far away
- मृधः (*mṛdhaḥ*)—destroy the violent enemies
- समुद्रात् (*samudrāt*)—from the one that supplies a flow of water
- बृहतः (*bṛhataḥ*)—and great
- आस्मात् दिवः (*āsmāt divaḥ*)—from this heaven
- अपां (*apāṃ*)—of the waters
- भूमानं (*bhūmānaṃ*)—the abundant streams
- नः (*naḥ*)—for us
- इह (*iha*)—to this world
- उप सृज (*upa sṛja*)—supply

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Agni, destroy the enemy strongholds that others cannot enter. Destroy disease. From heaven, which supplies a flow of water and is great, supply for us an abundant flow of water to this world.

##### English translation
Demolish, Agni, our enemies, demolish their strongholds, drive away disease, drive away the Rakshasas: from this ocean, from vast heaven send down upon us here abundance of rain.

### Printed p. 119
## Sūkta 99 (Maṇḍala 10)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> कं न इति द्वादशर्चं नवमं सूक्तं । वैखानसस्य वम्रस्यार्षं त्रैष्टुभमैंद्रं । तथा चानुक्रांतं । कं नो वम्रो वैखानस इति । गतो विनियोगः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The ninth sūkta, "कं नः (*kaṃ naḥ*)", has twelve ṛks. Its seer is Vamra, son of Vikhanas (Vaikhānasa); its deity is Indra; its metre is Triṣṭubh. The Anukramaṇī says: "Kaṃ no Vamro Vaikhānasaḥ." The viniyoga has been given already.
**NOTE**

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta "कं नः (*kaṃ naḥ*)" is the ninth sūkta in the eighth anuvāka. It has twelve ṛks. Its seer is Vamra, the son of the sage Vikhanas. Indra is the deity. The metre is Triṣṭubh. In the Anukramaṇī it is said: "Kaṃ no Vamro Vaikhānasaḥ." The viniyoga has already been stated earlier.

##### Particulars

- Maṇḍala 10; Anuvāka 8; Sūkta 99
- Aṣṭaka 8; Adhyāya 5; Varga 14, 15
- Number of ṛks in the sūkta: 12
- Ṛṣi: Vamra Vaikhānasa
- Deity: Indra
- Metre: Anuṣṭubh

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> कं न॑श्चि॒त्रमि॑षण्यसि चिकि॒त्वान्पृ॑थु॒ग्मानं॑ वा॒श्रं वा॑वृ॒धध्यै॑  
> कत्तस्य॒ दातु॒ शव॑सो॒ व्यु॑ष्टौ॒ तक्ष॒द्वज्रं॑ वृत्र॒तुर॒मपि॑न्वत् ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> कं । नः । चित्रं । इषण्यसि । चिकित्वान् । पृथुग्मानं । वाश्रं । ववृधध्यै ।  
> कत् । तस्य । दातु ! शवसः । विउष्टौ । तक्षत् । वज्रं । वृत्रतुरं । अपिन्वत् ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र नोऽस्माकं चित्रं चायनीयं कं धनविशेषमिषण्यसि । प्रेरयसि ।
> चिकित्सान् सर्वथा प्रेरणीयमिति जानन् । कीदृशं तं । पृथुग्धानं पृथुभावं

### Printed p. 120

> प्राप्नुवंतं वाश्रं शब्दनीयं स्तुत्यं । किमर्थं च । ववृधध्या अस्माकं वर्धनाय !
> किंच तस्येंद्रस्य शवसो बलस्य व्युष्टौ व्युच्छने सति कद्दातु किं दानमस्माकं
> भवतीति शेषः । यं वज्रं वृत्रतुरं वृत्रस्यावरकस्य पापस्य हिंसकं त्वष्टा तक्षत्
> आलक्ष्मि साधु संपादितवान् अपिन्वत् असिंचच्च! तस्य शवसो व्युष्टाविति संबंधः ।
> यद्वा! हे इंद्र कत्किं तस्य वज्रस्य दानं भवति यं वज्रमिंद्रार्थं तक्षदिति योजता
> मह्यं त्वष्टा वज्रमतक्षदायसं । ऋ. १०-४८-३ । इति मंत्रांतरं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya
